be a TBox and let
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
be obtained from
\mathcal{T}
by applying any number of rules from (
30
)–(
36
).
Let
\bar{C}
be a top-level culprit for
\tau\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
.
Then there is a constant number of rule applications leading to a TBox
\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}
that is faithful to
\mathcal{T}
and where
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}}(\bar{C})>\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}}(\bar{C})
.

Proof of Claim
.

We do a case distinction on the occurrence (left-hand vs. right-hand side) and form of
\bar{C}
.
In every case, we will explicitly give the (constantly many) rules to apply to decrease the cardinality of
\bar{C}
.
In most cases, it is easy to see that only one rule is needed, we show this exemplarily for one case and then concentrate on the two non-trivial cases.

•

\bar{C}=\exists R.\bar{D}
occurs on the right-hand side:
Then we apply rule (
30
), removing one occurrence of
\bar{C}
.
The resulting
\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}
is faithful because the only newly introduced concept terms are
\exists R.A
on a right-hand side and
A
on a left-hand side, and neither of these is a culprit.

•

\bar{C}=D_{1}\sqcap D_{2}
or
\bar{C}=\mathord{\mathop{\odot}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}D
and
\bar{C}
occurs on the right-hand side: Similar.

•

\bar{C}=\exists R.\bar{D}
or
\bar{C}=\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}D
and
\bar{C}
occurs on the left-hand side: Exercise.

•

\bar{C}=D_{1}\sqcap D_{2}
occurs on the left-hand side:
Denote
\tau=\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\bar{C}\sqsubseteq E]=\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[D_{1}\sqcap D_{2}\sqsubseteq E]
.
We apply rule (
34
) and obtain
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[D_{1}\sqsubseteq A]
and
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqcap D_{2}\sqsubseteq E]
, thus removing once occurence of
\bar{C}
.
The latter rule contains the (new) culprit
A\sqcap D_{2}
to which we can apply the same rule again (modulo commutativity) to obtain
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[D_{2}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}]
and
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqcap A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq E]
.
The only remaining (potential) culprits are
D_{1}
,
D_{2}
, and
E
, whence the resulting
\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}
is faithful to
\mathcal{T}
.

•

\bar{C}=\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}D
occurs on the left-hand side:
Denote
\tau=\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}D\sqsubseteq E]
.
We apply rules (to underlined GCIs) as follows:

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{\leadsto}}\left\{\underline{\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}D\sqsubseteq E]}\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(\ref{norm2:box-left})}}}{{\leadsto}}\left\{\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[D\sqsubseteq A_{1}],\underline{\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{v}_{0}}}A_{1}\sqcap\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}}A_{1}\sqsubseteq E]}\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(\ref{norm2:conjunction-left})}}}{{\leadsto}}\left\{\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[D\sqsubseteq A_{1}],\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{v}_{0}}}A_{1}\sqsubseteq A_{2}],\underline{\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A_{2}\sqcap\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}}A_{1}\sqsubseteq E]}\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(\ref{norm2:conjunction-left})}}}{{\leadsto}}\left\{\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[D\sqsubseteq A_{1}],\underline{\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{v}_{0}}}A_{1}\sqsubseteq A_{2}]},\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}}A_{1}\sqsubseteq A_{3}],\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A_{2}\sqcap A_{3}\sqsubseteq E]\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(\ref{norm2:diamond-left})}}}{{\leadsto}}\left\{\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[D\sqsubseteq A_{1}],\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\mathsf{v}_{0}}}}[A_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}A_{4}],\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A_{4}\sqsubseteq A_{2}],\underline{\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}}A_{1}\sqsubseteq A_{3}]},\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A_{2}\sqcap A_{3}\sqsubseteq E]\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(\ref{norm2:diamond-left})}}}{{\leadsto}}\left\{\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[D\sqsubseteq A_{1}],\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\mathsf{v}_{0}}}}[A_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}A_{4}],\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A_{4}\sqsubseteq A_{2}],\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\mathsf{v}_{1}}}}[A_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}A_{5}],\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A_{5}\sqsubseteq A_{3}],\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A_{2}\sqcap A_{3}\sqsubseteq E]\right\}

It is easy to see that
D
and
E
are the only remaining (potential) culprits, and that one occurence of
\bar{C}=\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}D
has been removed.

❑

Thus each culprit will be not only be removed eventually, but removing each single occurrence will take only a constant number of steps.
Therefore, the number of rule applications is linear in
\sum_{C\in\mathsf{SC}_{\mathcal{T}}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}(C)
.
By
1
, the number of rule applications is linear in
\left\lVert\mathcal{T}\right\rVert
, thus linear in
\left\lVert\mathcal{K}\right\rVert
.

It remains to show that the overall increase in size is at most linear.
We do this by showing that:

•

for each single rule that is applied to nested concept terms and can potentially be applied recursively, the size increase caused by its application is constant;

•

for each single rule that is applied only once to a statement (i.e., rule
25
), the size increase is at most linear.

Together with the overall linear number of rule applications, it follows that the size of the resulting normalised TBox
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
is at most linear in the size of the original TBox
\mathcal{T}
.

Rule (
24
):

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\left\lVert\mathsf{s}_{1}\cap\mathsf{s}_{2}\preceq\mathsf{s^{\prime}}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathsf{s^{\prime}}\cap\mathsf{s}_{3}\cap\ldots\cap\mathsf{s}_{n}\preceq\mathsf{s}\right\rVert-\left\lVert\mathsf{s}_{1}\cap\ldots\cap\mathsf{s}_{n}\preceq\mathsf{s}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=3+n-(n+1)

\displaystyle=2

Rule (
25
):

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\mathsf{s}_{1}}}}[\top\sqsubseteq A_{1}]\right\rVert+\ldots+\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\mathsf{s}_{n}}}}[\top\sqsubseteq A_{n}]\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[A_{1}\sqcap\ldots\sqcap A_{n}\sqsubseteq\bot]\right\rVert-\left\lVert\mathsf{s}_{1}\cap\ldots\cap\mathsf{s}_{n}\preceq\mathbf{0}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=4n+(n+3)-(n+1)

\displaystyle=4n+2

Rule (
26
):

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[R_{1}\circ R_{2}\sqsubseteq R^{\prime}]\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[R^{\prime}\circ R_{3}\circ\ldots\circ R_{n}\sqsubseteq R]\right\rVert-\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[R_{1}\circ\ldots\circ R_{n}\sqsubseteq R]\right\rVert

\displaystyle=4+(n+1)-(n+2)

\displaystyle=3

Rule (
27
):

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A(a)]\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqsubseteq\bar{C}]\right\rVert-\left(\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\bar{C}(a)]\right\rVert\right)

\displaystyle=3+3+\left\lVert\bar{C}\right\rVert-\left(1+\left\lVert\bar{C}\right\rVert+1\right)

\displaystyle 4

Rule (
28
):

Clear.

Rule (
29
):

Clear.

Rule (
30
):

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[B\sqsubseteq\exists R.A]\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqsubseteq\bar{C}]\right\rVert-\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[B\sqsubseteq\exists R.\bar{C}]\right\rVert

\displaystyle=1+1+\left\lVert B\right\rVert+2+1+1+1+\left\lVert\bar{C}\right\rVert-\left(1+1+\left\lVert B\right\rVert+1+\left\lVert\bar{C}\right\rVert\right)

\displaystyle=4

Rule (
31
):

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[B\sqsubseteq A]\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqsubseteq C]\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqsubseteq D]\right\rVert-\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[B\sqsubseteq C\sqcap D]\right\rVert

\displaystyle=(1+1+\left\lVert B\right\rVert+1)+(1+1+1+\left\lVert C\right\rVert)+(1+1+1+\left\lVert D\right\rVert)-\left(1+1+\left\lVert B\right\rVert+1+\left\lVert C\right\rVert+\left\lVert D\right\rVert\right)

\displaystyle=6

Rule (
32
):

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\odot}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}A]\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqsubseteq\bar{D}]\right\rVert-\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\odot}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}\bar{D}]\right\rVert

\displaystyle=1+1+\left\lVert C\right\rVert+2+1+1+1+\left\lVert\bar{D}\right\rVert-\left(1+1+\left\lVert C\right\rVert+1+\left\lVert\bar{D}\right\rVert\right)

\displaystyle=4

Rule (
33
):

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\bar{C}\sqsubseteq A]\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\exists R.A\sqsubseteq D]\right\rVert-\left\lVert\exists R.\bar{C}\sqsubseteq D\right\rVert

\displaystyle=1+1+\left\lVert\bar{C}\right\rVert+1+1+1+2+\left\lVert D\right\rVert-\left(1+1+\left\lVert\bar{C}\right\rVert+\left\lVert D\right\rVert\right)

\displaystyle=5

Rule (
34
):

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\bar{C}\sqsubseteq A]\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqcap D\sqsubseteq E]\right\rVert-\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\bar{C}\sqcap D\sqsubseteq E]\right\rVert

\displaystyle=1+1+\left\lVert\bar{C}\right\rVert+1+1+1+1+1+\left\lVert D\right\rVert+\left\lVert E\right\rVert-\left(1+1+1+\left\lVert\bar{C}\right\rVert+\left\lVert D\right\rVert+\left\lVert E\right\rVert\right)

\displaystyle=4

Rule (
35
):

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}A]\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqsubseteq D]\right\rVert-\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}C\sqsubseteq D]\right\rVert

\displaystyle=1+1+\left\lVert C\right\rVert+2+1+1+1+\left\lVert D\right\rVert-(1+1+1+\left\lVert C\right\rVert+\left\lVert D\right\rVert)

\displaystyle=4

Rule (
36
):

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\left\lVert\mathsf{v}_{0}\preceq\mathsf{u}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathsf{v}_{1}\preceq\mathsf{u}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[C\sqsubseteq A]\right\rVert+\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{v}_{0}}}A\sqcap\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}}A\sqsubseteq D]\right\rVert-\left\lVert\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}C\sqsubseteq D]\right\rVert

\displaystyle=2+2+1+1+\left\lVert C\right\rVert+1+1+1+2+2+\left\lVert D\right\rVert-(1+1+1+\left\lVert C\right\rVert+\left\lVert D\right\rVert)

\displaystyle=10

2.

Assume that
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
is the result of exhaustively applying the normalisation rules (
17
)–(
36
) to
\mathcal{K}
.
The proof is by contradiction.
Assume that a GCI
\mathord{\mathop{\odot}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[C\sqsubseteq D]
is not in normal form;
we do a case distinction on the possible reasons for this where in each case it will turn out that at least one of the rules is applicable in contradiction to the presumption.

•

\mathord{\mathop{\odot}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}\neq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}
: Then
\mathord{\mathop{\odot}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}=\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}
and Rule (
17
) is applicable.

•

D
is of the form
\exists R.E
with
E\notin\mathsf{BC}_{\mathcal{K}}\cup\left\{\bot\right\}
: Then Rule (
30
) is applicable.

•

D
is of the form
E_{1}\sqcap E_{2}
: Then Rule (
31
) is applicable.

•

D
is of the form
\mathord{\mathop{\odot}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}E
with
E\notin\mathsf{BC}_{\mathcal{K}}\cup\left\{\bot\right\}
: Then Rule (
32
) is applicable.

•

D=\top
or
C=\bot
: Then Rule (
28
) or Rule (
29
) is applicable.

•

C
is of the form
\exists R.B
with
B\notin\mathsf{BC}_{\mathcal{K}}
: Then Rule (
33
) is applicable.

•

C
is of the form
B_{1}\sqcap B_{2}
with
\left\{B_{1},B_{2}\right\}\not\subseteq\mathsf{BC}_{\mathcal{K}}
: Then Rule (
34
) is applicable.

•

C
is of the form
\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B
with
B\notin\mathsf{BC}_{\mathcal{K}}
: Then Rule (
35
) is applicable.

•

C
is of the form
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B
with
B\notin\mathsf{BC}_{\mathcal{K}}
: Then Rule (
36
) is applicable.

3.

We show correctness of each rule.
Correctness of the overall normalisation process follows by induction on the number of rule applications.
We slightly adapt the notation to denote by
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}=\mbox{$\left\langle\mathcal{S},\mathcal{T}^{\prime},\mathcal{A}\right\rangle$}
the KB that results from application
of a single rule
and do a case distinction on the rules.
In each case, assume
\mathfrak{D}=\mbox{$\left\langle\Delta,\Pi,\gamma,\sigma\right\rangle$}
with
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathcal{K}
and denote
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}=\mbox{$\left\langle\Delta^{\prime},\Pi^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\right\rangle$}
in case the components differ from those of
\mathfrak{D}
.
(In most cases, we only need to show
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
and therefore do not mention the other KB components.)
We start with phase 1 rules.

Rule (
17
)

(a)
Let
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\mu]
.
Then there is a
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s})
such that
\mathfrak{D},\pi\models\mu
.
Define
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}
from
\mathfrak{D}
by
\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{u})\mathrel{\,:=\,}\left\{\pi\right\}
.
It follows that
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathsf{u}\preceq\mathsf{s}
and
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[\mu]
.

(b)
Let
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
and consider any
\pi\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{u})
(which exists due to standpoint-non-emptiness).
Clearly
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
shows
\pi\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{u})\subseteq\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})
, whence
\mathfrak{D},\pi\models\mu
witnesses that
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\mu]
.

Rule (
18
)

(a)
Let
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\lambda_{1}\mathrel{\wedge}\ldots\mathrel{\wedge}\lambda_{n}]
.
Then for every
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s})
and
1\leq i\leq n
, we have
\mathfrak{D},\pi\models\lambda_{i}
.
Thus also
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\lambda_{i}]
for all
1\leq i\leq n
.

(b)
Let
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\lambda_{i}]
for all
1\leq i\leq n
.
Then for all
\pi\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})
and all
1\leq i\leq n
, we have
\mathfrak{D},\pi\models\lambda_{i}
.
Therefore, for all
\pi\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})
we have
\mathfrak{D},\pi\models\lambda_{1}\mathrel{\wedge}\ldots\mathrel{\wedge}\lambda_{n}
and thus
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\lambda_{1}\mathrel{\wedge}\ldots\mathrel{\wedge}\lambda_{n}]
.

Rule (
19
)

(a)
Let
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\neg(C\sqsubseteq D)]
, that is, for all
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s})
assume
\mathfrak{D},\pi\not\models C\sqsubseteq D
.
Then, in every
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s})
there is some
\delta_{\pi}\in\Delta
such that
\delta_{\pi}\in C^{\gamma(\pi)}\setminus D^{\gamma(\pi)}
.
Define
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}
from
\mathfrak{D}
as follows:
For every
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s})
set
A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}\mathrel{\,:=\,}\left\{\delta_{\pi}\right\}
and
R^{\prime\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}\mathrel{\,:=\,}\left\{\mbox{$\left\langle\varepsilon,\delta_{\pi}\right\rangle$}\;\middle|\;\varepsilon\in\Delta\right\}
.
Now for every
\pi\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})=\sigma(\mathsf{s})
:
We have
\delta_{\pi}\in A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
and
\delta_{\pi}\in C^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
whence
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqsubseteq C]
.
We have
\delta_{\pi}\notin D^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
whence
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqcap D\sqsubseteq\bot]
.
We have
\varepsilon\in\Delta
implies
\mbox{$\left\langle\varepsilon,\delta_{\pi}\right\rangle$}\in R^{\prime\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
and
\delta_{\pi}\in A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
whence
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\exists R^{\prime}.A]
.

(b)
Let
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqsubseteq C]\mathrel{\wedge}\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqcap D\sqsubseteq\bot]\mathrel{\wedge}\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\exists R^{\prime}.A]
and let
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})
be arbitrary.
Then by
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\top\sqsubseteq\exists R^{\prime}.A
and
\Delta^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
we get
A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}\neq\emptyset
.
Thus assume
\delta^{\prime}\in A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.
By
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqsubseteq C]
we get
\delta^{\prime}\in C^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.
By
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqcap D\sqsubseteq\bot]
we get
\delta^{\prime}\notin D^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.
Therefore, for every
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})
, we find a
\delta^{\prime}\in C^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}\setminus D^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
;
we conclude that
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\neg(C\sqsubseteq D)]
.

Rule (
20
)

(a)
Let
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\neg C(a)]
, that is, for every
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s})
assume
a^{\mathfrak{D}}\notin C^{\gamma(\pi)}
.
Define
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}
from
\mathfrak{D}
as follows:
For every
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s})
, set
A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}\mathrel{\,:=\,}\left\{a^{\mathfrak{D}}\right\}
.
Then clearly
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A(a)]
, and due to the above also
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqcap C\sqsubseteq\bot]
.

(b)
Let
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqcap C\sqsubseteq\bot]\mathrel{\wedge}\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A(a)]
.
Then in every
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})
, we have
a^{\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}}\in A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
and
A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}\cap C^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}=\emptyset
, that is,
a^{\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}}\notin C^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.

Rule (
21
)

(a)
Let
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\neg R(a,b)]
, that is, for every
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s})
assume
\mathfrak{D},\pi\not\models R(a,b)
(i.e.
\mbox{$\left\langle a^{\mathfrak{D}},b^{\mathfrak{D}}\right\rangle$}\notin R^{\gamma(\pi)}
).
Define
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}
from
\mathfrak{D}
as follows:
For every
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s})
, set
A_{a}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}\mathrel{\,:=\,}\left\{a^{\mathfrak{D}}\right\}
and
A_{b}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}\mathrel{\,:=\,}\left\{b^{\mathfrak{D}}\right\}
.
We then have
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A_{a}(a)]
,
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A_{b}(b)]
, and
a^{\mathfrak{D}}\notin(\exists R.A_{b})^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.

(b)
Let
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A_{b}(b)]\mathrel{\wedge}\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A_{a}\sqcap\exists R.A_{b}\sqsubseteq\bot]\mathrel{\wedge}\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A_{a}(a)]
.
Then for every
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})
:

b^{\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}}\in A_{b}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
,
a^{\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}}\in A_{a}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
, and for all
\delta\in A_{a}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
we find
\delta\notin(\exists R.A_{b})^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
, thus in particular
a^{\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}}\notin(\exists R.A_{b})^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
and
\mbox{$\left\langle a^{\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}},b^{\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}}\right\rangle$}\notin R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.

Rule (
22
)

(a)
Let
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\neg(R_{1}\circ\ldots\circ R_{n}\sqsubseteq R)]
, that is, in every
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s})
there exist
\delta_{0},\delta_{1},\ldots,\delta_{n}\in\Delta
such that for all
1\leq i\leq n
,
\mbox{$\left\langle\delta_{i-1},\delta_{i}\right\rangle$}\in R_{i}^{\gamma(\pi)}
, but
\mbox{$\left\langle\delta_{0},\delta_{n}\right\rangle$}\notin R^{\gamma(\pi)}
.
Define
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}
from
\mathfrak{D}
as follows:
For every
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s})
, set
A_{a}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}\mathrel{\,:=\,}\left\{\delta_{0}\right\}
,
A_{b}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}\mathrel{\,:=\,}\left\{\delta_{n}\right\}
, and
R^{\prime\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}\mathrel{\,:=\,}\left\{\mbox{$\left\langle\varepsilon,\delta_{0}\right\rangle$}\;\middle|\;\varepsilon\in\Delta\right\}
.
It follows by construction that
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\exists R^{\prime}.A_{a}]
, and
a^{\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}}\notin(\exists R.A_{b})^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
whence
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A_{a}\sqcap\exists R.A_{b}\sqsubseteq\bot]
.
We can also show for each
\pi\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})=\sigma(\mathsf{s})
and for all
1\leq i\leq n
(by induction on
i
), that we have
\delta_{i-1}\in(\exists R_{i}.\cdots\exists R_{n}.A_{b})^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
, whence in particular
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A_{a}\sqsubseteq\exists R_{1}.\cdots\exists R_{n}.A_{b}]
.

(b)
Let
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\exists R^{\prime}.A_{a}]\mathrel{\wedge}\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A_{a}\sqcap\exists R.A_{b}\sqsubseteq\bot]\mathrel{\wedge}\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A_{a}\sqsubseteq\exists R_{1}.\cdots\exists R_{n}.A_{b}]
and consider any
\pi\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})
.
From
\Delta^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
and
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\exists R^{\prime}.A_{a}]
we get
A_{a}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}\neq\emptyset
, say
\delta_{0}\in A_{a}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.
Therefore, from
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A_{a}\sqsubseteq\exists R_{1}.\cdots\exists R_{n}.A_{b}]
we get that there exist
\delta_{1},\ldots,\delta_{n}
such that
\mbox{$\left\langle\delta_{0},\delta_{1}\right\rangle$}\in R_{1}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
, …, and
\mbox{$\left\langle\delta_{n-1},\delta_{n}\right\rangle$}\in R_{n}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
where
\delta_{n}\in A_{b}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.
By
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A_{a}\sqcap\exists R.A_{b}\sqsubseteq\bot]
we get that
\mbox{$\left\langle\delta_{0},\delta_{n}\right\rangle$}\notin R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.
Therefore, for any
\pi\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})
we find
\mbox{$\left\langle\delta_{0},\delta_{n}\right\rangle$}\in(R_{1}\circ\ldots\circ R_{n})^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}\setminus R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.

Rule (
23
)

(a)
Let
\mathfrak{D}\models\neg(\mathsf{s}_{1}\mathord{\,\cap}\ldots\mathord{\cap\,}\mathsf{s}_{n}\preceq\mathsf{s})
.
Then there is a
\pi^{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{s}_{1})\cap\ldots\cap\sigma(\mathsf{s}_{n})\setminus\sigma(\mathsf{s})
.
Construct
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}
from
\mathfrak{D}
by setting
\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{u})\mathrel{\,:=\,}\left\{\pi^{*}\right\}
.
It is clear that
\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{u})\subseteq\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s}_{i})
for all
1\leq i\leq n
;
furthermore
\pi^{*}\notin\sigma(\mathsf{s})
shows that
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathsf{u}\cap\mathsf{s}\preceq\mathbf{0}
.

(b)
Let
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathsf{u}\preceq\mathsf{s}_{1}\mathrel{\wedge}\ldots\mathrel{\wedge}\mathsf{u}\preceq\mathsf{s}_{n}\mathrel{\wedge}\mathsf{u}\cap\mathsf{s}\preceq\mathbf{0}
.
By non-emptiness of
\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{u})
, there is some
\pi_{*}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{u})
.
Clearly
\pi_{*}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s}_{i})
for all
1\leq i\leq n
, whence
\pi_{*}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s}_{1})\cap\ldots\cap\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s}_{n})
.
By
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathsf{u}\cap\mathsf{s}\preceq\mathbf{0}
we furthermore get
\pi_{*}\notin\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})
.

We conclude with correctness of phase 2 rules.

Rule (
24
):

(a)
Straightforward, we interpret
\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})\mathrel{\,:=\,}(\sigma(\mathsf{s}_{1})\cap\sigma(\mathsf{s}_{2}))\cup\left\{\pi_{\mathsf{s}}\right\}
for some arbitrary
\pi_{\mathsf{s}}\in\sigma(\mathsf{s})
.

(b) Clear.

Rule (
25
):

(a)
Let
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathsf{s}_{1}\cap\ldots\cap\mathsf{s}_{n}\preceq\mathbf{0}
.
It is clear that in
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}
we must define, for all
1\leq i\leq n
and
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s}_{i})
,
A_{i}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}\mathrel{\,:=\,}\Delta
, to satisfy the axioms of the form
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\mathsf{s}_{i}}}}[\top\sqsubseteq A_{i}]
.
Assume for the sake of obtaining a contradiction that
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\not\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[A_{1}\sqcap\ldots\sqcap A_{n}\sqsubseteq\bot]
.
Then there is a
\pi\in\Pi
and some
\delta\in\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta
such that
\delta\in A_{i}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
for all
1\leq i\leq n
.
But by construction this means that
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s}_{1})\cap\ldots\cap\sigma(\mathsf{s}_{n})
, a contradiction to the presumption.
Thus
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[A_{1}\sqcap\ldots\sqcap A_{n}\sqsubseteq\bot]
.

(b)
Let
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\mathsf{s}_{1}}}}[\top\sqsubseteq A_{1}]\mathrel{\wedge}\ldots\mathrel{\wedge}\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\mathsf{s}_{n}}}}[\top\sqsubseteq A_{n}]\mathrel{\wedge}\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[A_{1}\sqcap\ldots\sqcap A_{n}\sqsubseteq\bot]
.
The proof works as in direction (a).

Rule (
26
):

(a)
Straightforward, we interpret
R^{\prime\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}\mathrel{\,:=\,}R_{1}^{\gamma(\pi)}\circ R_{2}^{\gamma(\pi)}
.

(b)
Clear.

Rule (
27
):

(a)
Let
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\bar{C}(a)]
.
In
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}
we define
A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}\mathrel{\,:=\,}\bar{C}^{\gamma(\pi)}
and
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A(a)]\mathrel{\wedge}\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqsubseteq\bar{C}]
follows.

(b)
Clear.

Rules (
28
) and (
29
):

Clear, as any DL interpretation satisfies
C\sqsubseteq\top
and
\bot\sqsubseteq D
.

Rule (
30
):

(a)
Let
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[B\sqsubseteq\exists R.\bar{C}]
.
Define
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}
from
\mathfrak{D}
as follows:
For any
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s})
, set
A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}\mathrel{\,:=\,}\bar{C}^{\gamma(\pi)}
.
It follows by definition that
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[B\sqsubseteq\exists R.A]
and
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqsubseteq\bar{C}]
.

(b)
Let
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
and consider any
\pi\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})
and
\delta\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.
By
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[B\sqsubseteq\exists R.A]
we get
\delta\in(\exists R.A)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
, whence there is an
\varepsilon\in\Delta^{\prime}
with
\mbox{$\left\langle\delta,\varepsilon\right\rangle$}\in r^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
and
\varepsilon\in A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.
By
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqsubseteq\bar{C}]
, we get
\varepsilon\in\bar{C}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
and ultimately
\delta\in(\exists R.\bar{C})^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.

Rule (
31
):

(a)
Let
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[B\sqsubseteq C\sqcap D]
and define
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}
from
\mathfrak{D}
by setting, for each
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s})
,
A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}\mathrel{\,:=\,}B^{\gamma(\pi)}
.
Then by definition we get
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[B\sqsubseteq A]
, as well as
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqsubseteq C]
and
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqsubseteq D]
.

(b)
Let
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
and consider any
\pi\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})
and
\delta\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.
By
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[B\sqsubseteq A]
we get
\delta\in A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.
By
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqsubseteq C]
and
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqsubseteq D]
we get
\delta\in C^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
and
\delta\in D^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
, respectively.
In combination,
\delta\in(C\sqcap D)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.

Rule (
32
):

(a)
Let
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\odot}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}\bar{D}]
and define
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}
from
\mathfrak{D}
by setting, for each
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s})
,
A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}\mathrel{\,:=\,}\bar{D}^{\gamma(\pi)}
.
It follows directly that
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\odot}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}A]
and
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqsubseteq\bar{D}]
.

(b)
Let
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
and consider any
\pi\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})
and
\delta\in C^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.
From
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\odot}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}A]
we get
\delta\in(\mathord{\mathop{\odot}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}A)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.
Thus, for some (all)
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{u})
, we get
\delta\in A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
;
and in turn, by
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqsubseteq\bar{D}]
, for some (all)
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{u})
, we get
\delta\in\bar{D}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.

Rule (
33
):

(a)
Let
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\exists R.\bar{C}\sqsubseteq D]
.
Define
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}
from
\mathfrak{D}
as follows:
For any
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s})
, set
A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}\mathrel{\,:=\,}\bar{C}^{\gamma(\pi)}
.
It follows from this definition that
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\bar{C}\sqsubseteq A]
and
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\exists R.A\sqsubseteq D]
.

(b)
Let
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
and consider
\pi\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})
and any
\delta\in\exists R.\bar{C}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.
There is thus a
\varepsilon\in\Delta
with
\mbox{$\left\langle\delta,\varepsilon\right\rangle$}\in r^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
and
\varepsilon\in\bar{C}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.
By
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\bar{C}\sqsubseteq A]
we get
\varepsilon\in A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
, and in turn
\delta\in(\exists R.A)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.
By
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\exists R.A\sqsubseteq D]
, we get
\delta\in D^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.

Rule (
34
):

(a)
Let
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\bar{C}\sqcap D\sqsubseteq E]
.
Define
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}
from
\mathfrak{D}
as follows:
For any
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s})
, set
A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}\mathrel{\,:=\,}\bar{C}^{\gamma(\pi)}
.
It follows from this definition that
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\bar{C}\sqsubseteq A]
and
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqcap D\sqsubseteq E]
.

(b)
Let
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
and consider
\pi\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})
and
\delta\in\bar{C}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}\cap D^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.
By
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\bar{C}\sqsubseteq A]
we get
\delta\in A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
, and
by
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqcap D\sqsubseteq E]
and
\delta\in D^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
we get
\delta\in E^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.

Rule (
35
):

(a)
Let
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}C\sqsubseteq D]
and define
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}
from
\mathfrak{D}
by setting
A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}\mathrel{\,:=\,}(\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}C)^{\gamma(\pi)}
for every
\pi\in\Pi
.

•

\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}A]
:
Let
\pi\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{u})=\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in C^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}=C^{\gamma(\pi)}
.
Then by definition, we have
\delta\in A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
for all
\pi\in\Pi
, whence
\delta\in(\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}A)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.

•

\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqsubseteq D]
:
Let
\pi\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})=\sigma(\mathsf{s})
and
\delta\in A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.
Then by definition,
\delta\in(\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}C)^{\gamma(\pi)}
and by the presumption that
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}C\sqsubseteq D]
we get
\delta\in D^{\gamma(\pi)}=D^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.

(b)
Let
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
and consider
\pi\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})
and
\delta\in(\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}C)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.
Then there is a
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{u})
such that
\delta\in C^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.
Thus
\delta\in(\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}A)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
, that is,
\delta\in\bigcap_{\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\Pi}A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime\prime})}
.
Thus in particular for
\pi\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})
, we get
\delta\in A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
and by
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[A\sqsubseteq D]
we obtain
\delta\in D^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi)}
.

Rule (
36
):

(a) Let
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}C\sqsubseteq D]
.
We define
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}=\mbox{$\left\langle\Delta^{\prime},\Pi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\right\rangle$}
as follows:

•

\Delta^{\prime}
consists of two copies
\delta^{\prime}_{0},\delta^{\prime}_{1}
of every function
\delta^{\prime}\colon\sigma(\mathsf{u})\times\Delta\to\Delta
.

•

\Pi^{\prime}
consists of all functions
\pi^{\prime}\colon\sigma(\mathsf{u})\times\Delta\to\Pi
plus two extra, distinct copies of the particular function
\pi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{diag}}\mathrel{\,:=\,}\left\{(\pi,\delta)\mapsto\pi\right\}
, denoted
\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}}
and
\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}
.

•

\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{v}_{b})\mathrel{\,:=\,}\left\{\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{b}}\right\}
for each
b\in\left\{0,1\right\}
and, for all other
\mathsf{s}
,

\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})\mathrel{\,:=\,}\left\{\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi^{\prime}\;\middle|\;\mathop{\bigcup_{\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}),}}_{\delta\in\Delta}\left\{\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\right\}\subseteq\sigma(\mathsf{s})\right\}

For every
\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi^{\prime}
, the DL interpretation
\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})
over
\Delta^{\prime}
is such that

\displaystyle a^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\,:=\,}\left\{(\pi,\delta)\mapsto\delta_{a}\right\}_{0}

\displaystyle\text{ for }a\in N_{\mathsf{I}}

where
\delta_{a}\in\Delta
denotes the domain element for which
\delta_{a}=a^{\gamma(\pi)}
for all
\pi\in\Pi
.

Further, for
\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi^{\prime}\setminus\{\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}},\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}\}
, the DL interpretation
\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})
over
\Delta^{\prime}
is such that

\displaystyle A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\,:=\,}\left\{\delta^{\prime}_{b}\in\Delta^{\prime}\;\middle|\;\delta^{\prime}_{b}(\pi,\delta)\in A^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}\text{ for all }{\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})},\ {\delta\in\Delta}\right\}

\displaystyle\text{ for }A\in N_{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\,:=\,}\left\{\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{b},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{b}\right\rangle$}\in\Delta^{\prime}\times\Delta^{\prime}\;\middle|\;\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{b}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{b}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}\text{ for all }{\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})},\ {\delta\in\Delta}\right\}

\displaystyle\text{ for }R\in N_{\mathsf{R}}

and
\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{b}})
assigns as follows:

\displaystyle A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{b}})}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\,:=\,}\left(\left\{\delta^{\prime}_{b}\in\Delta^{\prime}\right\}\cap A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{diag}})}\right)\cup\left\{\delta^{\prime}_{1-b}\in\Delta^{\prime}\;\middle|\;\bigcup_{{\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}),}\atop{\delta\in\Delta}}\left\{\delta^{\prime}_{1-b}(\pi,\delta)\right\}\subseteq{\bigcap_{\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})}}A^{\gamma(\pi)}\right\}

\displaystyle\text{ for }A\in N_{\mathsf{C}}

\displaystyle R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{b}})}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\,:=\,}\left(\left\{\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{b},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{b}\right\rangle$}\in\Delta^{\prime}\times\Delta^{\prime}\right\}\cap R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{diag}})}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cup\left\{\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{1-b},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1-b}\right\rangle$}\in\Delta^{\prime}\times\Delta^{\prime}\;\middle|\;\bigcup_{{\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}),}\atop{\delta\in\Delta}}\left\{\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{1-b}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1-b}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\right\}\subseteq{\bigcap_{\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})}}R^{\gamma(\pi)}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\cup\left\{\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{1-b},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{b}\right\rangle$}\in\Delta^{\prime}\times\Delta^{\prime}\;\middle|\;\mbox{$\left\langle\zeta^{\prime}_{b},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{b}\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{diag}})}\text{ for some }\zeta^{\prime}_{b}\approx\delta^{\prime}_{1-b}\right\}

\displaystyle\text{ for }R\in N_{\mathsf{R}}

where
\zeta^{\prime}_{b}\approx\delta^{\prime}_{1-b}
holds iff for every
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
we have

\textstyle\bigcup_{{\delta\in\Delta}}\{\zeta^{\prime}_{b}(\pi,\delta)\}=\textstyle\bigcup_{{\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}),}\atop{\delta_{*}\in\Delta}}\{\delta^{\prime}_{1-b}(\pi_{*},\delta_{*})\}
.
Finally for the fresh concept name
A
introduced by the rule, define
A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}\mathrel{\,:=\,}C^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
for all
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{u})
.

We now show that
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
.
For this, we start out with some useful observations.

Claim 3
.

For every concept
E
,
\delta^{\prime}\in\Delta^{\prime}
, and
\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi^{\prime}\setminus\left\{\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}},\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}\right\}
:

\delta^{\prime}\in E^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}\iff\left(\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in E^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}\right)

Proof of Claim
.

We use structural induction on
E
.

•

E=A\in\mathsf{BC}_{\mathcal{K}}
: By definition.

•

E=E_{1}\sqcap E_{2}
:

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{\iff}}\delta^{\prime}\in(E_{1}\sqcap E_{2})^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\iff\delta^{\prime}\in E_{1}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}\cap E_{2}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\iff\delta^{\prime}\in E_{1}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}\text{ and }\delta^{\prime}\in E_{2}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(IH)}}}{{\iff}}\left[\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in E_{1}^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}\right]\text{ and }\left[\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in E_{2}^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}\right]

\displaystyle\iff\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\left[\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in E_{1}^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}\text{ and }\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in E_{2}^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}\right]

\displaystyle\iff\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in E_{1}^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}\cap E_{2}^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}

\displaystyle\iff\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in(E_{1}\sqcap E_{2})^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}

•

E=\exists R.B
:

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{\iff}}\delta^{\prime}\in\exists R.B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\iff\exists\varepsilon^{\prime}\in\Delta^{\prime}:\left[\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime}\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}\text{ and }\varepsilon^{\prime}\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(IH)}}}{{\iff}}\exists\varepsilon^{\prime}\in\Delta^{\prime}:\left[\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime}\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}\text{ and }\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\varepsilon^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}\right]

\displaystyle\iff\exists\varepsilon^{\prime}\in\Delta^{\prime}:\left[\left[\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}\right]\text{ and }\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\varepsilon^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}\right]

\displaystyle\iff\exists\varepsilon^{\prime}\in\Delta^{\prime}:\left[\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\left[\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}\text{ and }\varepsilon^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ddagger}}{{\iff}}\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\exists\varepsilon^{\prime}\in\Delta^{\prime}:\left[\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}\text{ and }\varepsilon^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\dagger}}{{\iff}}\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\exists\varepsilon\in\Delta:\left[\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}\text{ and }\varepsilon\in B^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}\right]

\displaystyle\iff\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in(\exists R.B)^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}

Note that for the “
\impliedby
” direction, there is always an
\varepsilon^{\prime}\in\Delta^{\prime}
such that for all
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in\Delta
we find
\varepsilon^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)=\varepsilon
for the appropriate
\varepsilon\in\Delta
(step
\dagger
), and thus this
\varepsilon^{\prime}
is in this sense independent of concrete
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in\Delta
(step
\ddagger
).

•

E=\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B
:

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{\iff}}\delta^{\prime}\in(\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\iff\exists\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}}):\delta^{\prime}\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime\prime})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(IH)}}}{{\iff}}\exists\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}}):\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime\prime}(\pi,\delta))}

\displaystyle\iff\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\exists\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}}):\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime\prime}(\pi,\delta))}

\displaystyle\iff\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\exists\pi_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}\in\sigma(\mathsf{s^{\prime}}):\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}})}

\displaystyle\iff\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in(\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B)^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}

Again, for “
\impliedby
”, we find a
\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\sigma(\mathsf{s^{\prime}})
for which always
\pi^{\prime\prime}(\pi,\delta)=\pi_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}
as desired.

•

E=\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B
:

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{\iff}}\delta^{\prime}\in(\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\iff\forall\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}}):\delta^{\prime}\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(IH)}}}{{\iff}}\forall\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}}):\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime\prime}(\pi,\delta))}

\displaystyle\iff\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}}):\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime\prime}(\pi,\delta))}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\dagger}}{{\iff}}\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}\in\sigma(\mathsf{s^{\prime}}):\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}})}

\displaystyle\iff\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in(\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B)^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}

The equivalence marked
\dagger
holds because for every
\pi_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}\in\sigma(\mathsf{s^{\prime}})
we can find a
\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}})
such that
\pi^{\prime\prime}(\pi,\delta)=\pi_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}
for all
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in\Delta
(e.g. the constant function
\left\{(\pi,\delta)\mapsto\pi_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}\right\}
);
conversely, for every
\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}})
,
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
, and
\delta\in\Delta
, we have
\pi^{\prime\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in\sigma(\mathsf{s^{\prime}})
by definition.

❑

The claim above does not consider the newly introduced precisifications.
They are however covered by the next claim.

Claim 4
.

For every concept
E
,
\delta^{\prime}\in\Delta^{\prime}
, and
\pi^{\prime}\in\left\{\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}},\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}\right\}
:

(a)

\delta^{\prime}\in E^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}\implies\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in E^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}

(b)

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in E^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi_{*},\delta))}\implies\delta^{\prime}\in E^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}

Proof of Claim
.

We use structural induction on
E
.

•

E=A\in\mathsf{BC}_{\mathcal{K}}
:
Assume
\pi^{\prime}=\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}}
(the remaining case is symmetric).
For direction (
3a
) we have:

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{\iff}}\delta^{\prime}_{b}\in A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}})}\text{ for some }b\in\left\{0,1\right\}

\displaystyle\iff\delta^{\prime}_{0}\in A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{diag}})}\text{ or }\mathop{\bigcup_{\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}),}}_{\delta\in\Delta}\left\{\delta^{\prime}_{1}(\pi,\delta)\right\}\subseteq\bigcap_{\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})}A^{\gamma(\pi)}

\displaystyle\iff\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta)\in A^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{diag}}(\pi,\delta))}\text{ or }\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\delta^{\prime}_{1}(\pi,\delta)\in A^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}

\displaystyle\iff\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta)\in A^{\gamma(\pi)}\text{ or }\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\delta^{\prime}_{1}(\pi,\delta)\in A^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}

\displaystyle\implies\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta)\in A^{\gamma(\pi)}\text{ or }\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}_{1}(\pi,\delta)\in A^{\gamma(\pi)}

\displaystyle\iff\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}_{b}(\pi,\delta)\in A^{\gamma(\pi)}\text{ for some }b\in\left\{0,1\right\}

\displaystyle\iff\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}_{b}(\pi,\delta)\in A^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{diag}}(\pi,\delta))}\text{ for some }b\in\left\{0,1\right\}

\displaystyle\iff\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}_{b}(\pi,\delta)\in A^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}}(\pi,\delta))}\text{ for some }b\in\left\{0,1\right\}

In direction (
3b
), we have:

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{\iff}}\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\delta^{\prime}_{b}(\pi,\delta)\in E^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi_{*},\delta))}\text{ for some }b\in\left\{0,1\right\}

\displaystyle\iff\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\delta^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta)\in E^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi_{*},\delta))}\text{ or }\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\delta^{\prime}_{1}(\pi,\delta)\in E^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi_{*},\delta))}

\displaystyle\implies\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta)\in A^{\gamma(\pi)}\text{ or }\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\delta^{\prime}_{1}(\pi,\delta)\in A^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}

In the last line, we continue with the equivalences from direction (
3a
) above the line with “
\implies\!
”.

•

E=\exists R.B
: We show the claim for
\pi^{\prime}=\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}}
, as the other case is symmetric.
In direction (
3a
), let
\delta^{\prime}_{b}\in(\exists R.B)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.
By definition of the semantics, there is a
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}\in\Delta^{\prime}
such that
\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{b},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.
Applying the induction hypothesis to
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
yields that

\displaystyle\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}=B^{\gamma(\pi)}

(41)

By definition of
R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
, we have three cases for
\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{b},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
, in each of which we show the claim.

(a)

\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{0},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{diag}})}
.
That is,
\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi)}
for all
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in\Delta
.
Together with
Equation

41
, we obtain

\forall\pi\in\sigma:\forall\delta\in\Delta:\left(\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi)}\text{ and }\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi)}\right)

Now since
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1}
are two distinct copies of the same function, this means that

\forall\pi\in\sigma:\forall\delta\in\Delta:\left(\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi)}\text{ and }\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi)}\right)

Thus

\forall\pi\in\sigma:\forall\delta\in\Delta:\exists\varepsilon\in\Delta:\left(\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi)}\text{ and }\varepsilon\in B^{\gamma(\pi)}\right)

namely we set
\varepsilon\mathrel{\,:=\,}\varepsilon^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)
in each case.
By the definition of the semantics we get

\forall\pi\in\sigma:\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in(\exists R.B)^{\gamma(\pi)}

which, by
\pi^{\prime}=\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}}=\pi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{diag}}
means

\forall\pi\in\sigma:\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in(\exists R.B)^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}

(b)

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{1}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}
.
In combination with
Equation

41
, we get

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\left(\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{1}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}\text{ and }\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi)}\right)

which, by virtue of
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1}
being the same function, means

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\left(\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{1}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}\text{ and }\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi)}\right)

which in particular implies

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\left(\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{1}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi)}\text{ and }\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi)}\right)

which implies the claim as in the case above.

(c)

there is some
\zeta^{\prime}_{0}\in\Delta^{\prime}
with
\zeta^{\prime}_{0}\approx\delta^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mbox{$\left\langle\zeta^{\prime}_{0},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{diag}})}
.
The latter property implies

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\mbox{$\left\langle\zeta^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi)}

In turn, from
\zeta^{\prime}_{0}\approx\delta^{\prime}_{0}
we obtain

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\bigcup_{\delta\in\Delta}\left\{\zeta^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta)\right\}=\mathop{\bigcup_{\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}),}}_{\delta_{*}\in\Delta}\left\{\delta^{\prime}_{1}(\pi_{*},\delta_{*})\right\}

This means in particular that

\forall\pi,\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta_{*}\in\Delta:\exists\delta_{+}\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}_{1}(\pi_{*},\delta_{*})=\zeta^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta_{+})

Thus

\forall\pi,\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta_{*}\in\Delta:\exists\delta_{+}\in\Delta:\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{1}(\pi_{*},\delta_{*}),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta_{+})\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi)}

which we combine with
Equation

41
as usual to obtain

\forall\pi,\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta_{*}\in\Delta:\exists\delta_{+}\in\Delta:\left(\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{1}(\pi_{*},\delta_{*}),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta_{+})\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi)}\text{ and }\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}(\pi,\delta_{+})\in B^{\gamma(\pi)}\right)

which in particular implies

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\exists\delta_{+}\in\Delta:\left(\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{1}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta_{+})\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi)}\text{ and }\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta_{+})\in B^{\gamma(\pi)}\right)

whence we get

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}_{1}(\pi,\delta)\in(\exists R.B)^{\gamma(\pi)}

In direction (
3b
), assume that

\forall\pi\in\sigma:\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\delta^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta)\in(\exists R.B)^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}},\delta))}

By the definition of the semantics, we obtain

\forall\pi\in\sigma:\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\exists\varepsilon\in\Delta:\left(\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi_{*},\delta))}\text{ and }\varepsilon_{\pi,\delta}\in B^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi_{*},\delta))}\right)

Since
\pi^{\prime}=\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}}=\pi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{diag}}
, this means that

\forall\pi\in\sigma:\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\exists\varepsilon_{\pi,\delta}\in\Delta:\left(\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}\text{ and }\varepsilon\in B^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}\right)

Since
\Delta^{\prime}
contains every function
\zeta^{\prime}\colon\sigma(\mathsf{u})\times\Delta\to\Delta
, there in particular exists a function
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}\in\Delta^{\prime}
such that
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta)=\varepsilon_{\pi,\delta}
for all
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in\Delta
.
Thus

\exists\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}\in\Delta^{\prime}:\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\left(\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}\text{ and }\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}\right)

which means

\left(\exists\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}\in\Delta^{\prime}:\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}\right)\text{ and }\\
\left(\exists\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}\in\Delta^{\prime}:\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}\right)

where we can apply the induction hypothesis to the second line to obtain

\left(\exists\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}\in\Delta^{\prime}:\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}\right)\text{ and }\\
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}})}

which in particular means that

\exists\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}\in\Delta^{\prime}:\left(\left(\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi)}\right)\text{ and }\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}})}\right)

which (employing among other things that
\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}}(\pi,\delta)=\pi
for all
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in\Delta
) implies

\exists\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}\in\Delta^{\prime}:\left(\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{0},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}})}\text{ and }\varepsilon^{\prime}\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}})}\right)

that is,

\delta^{\prime}_{0}\in(\exists R.B)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}})}
.

•

E=\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B
:
In direction (
3a
), assume
\delta^{\prime}\in(\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.
Thus there exists some
\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}})
such that
\delta^{\prime}\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime\prime})}
.
The induction hypothesis yields

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime\prime}(\pi,\delta))}

Thus for all
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in\Delta
there exists a
\pi_{\pi,\delta}^{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}\in\sigma(\mathsf{s^{\prime}})
, namely
\pi_{\pi,\delta}^{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}\mathrel{\,:=\,}\pi^{\prime\prime}(\pi,\delta)
, such that
\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi_{\pi,\delta}^{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}})}
.
This directly yields

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in(\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.

In direction (
3b
), assume

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in(\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B)^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}}(\pi_{*},\delta))}

This means that

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\exists\pi_{\pi_{*},\delta}^{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}\in\sigma(\mathsf{s^{\prime}}):\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi_{\pi_{*},\delta}^{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}})}

Choose
\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}})
such that for all
\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in\Delta
, we have
\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}(\pi_{*},\delta)=\pi_{\pi_{*},\delta}^{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}
.
Thus we obtain

\exists\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}}):\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}(\pi_{*},\delta))}

We apply the induction hypothesis and get

\exists\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}\in\Delta^{\prime}:\delta^{\prime}\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}})}
, that is,

\delta^{\prime}\in(\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.

•

E=\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B
:
In direction (
3a
), we have

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{\implies}}\delta^{\prime}\in(\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\implies\forall\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}}):\delta^{\prime}\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime\prime})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(IH)}}}{{\implies}}\forall\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}}):\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime\prime}(\pi,\delta))}

\displaystyle\implies\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}}):\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime\prime}(\pi,\delta))}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\dagger}}{{\implies}}\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}\in\sigma(\mathsf{s^{\prime}}):\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}})}

\displaystyle\implies\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in(\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B)^{\gamma(\pi)}

and for the converse direction, (
3b
), we consider

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{\implies}}\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in(\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}B)^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi_{*},\delta))}

\displaystyle\implies\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\pi_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}\in\sigma(\mathsf{s^{\prime}}):\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\dagger}}{{\implies}}\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}}):\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime\prime}(\pi,\delta))}

\displaystyle\implies\forall\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}}):\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime\prime}(\pi,\delta))}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(IH)}}}{{\implies}}\forall\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}}):\delta^{\prime}\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime\prime})}

\displaystyle\implies\delta^{\prime}\in(\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}

Here, in both directions the implications marked
\dagger
can be justifed just like in the proof of
3
.

❑

The two previous claims can be combined to obtain the following “global” property:

Claim 5
.

For every concept
E
,
\delta^{\prime}\in\Delta^{\prime}
, and
\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi^{\prime}
:

\delta^{\prime}\in E^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}\implies\left(\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in E^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}\right)

Proof of Claim
.

Follows from direction “
\implies\!
” of
3
and
Item

3a
of
4
.

❑

For the new precisifications, we sometimes also have to pay attention to which copy of the functions in
\Delta^{\prime}
we are currently considering, especially for the main case we will need below, namely where both copies are contained in the same concept’s extension in both precisifications.

Claim 6
.

For every concept
E
, function
\delta^{\prime}\colon\sigma(\mathsf{u})\times\Delta\to\Delta
, and
b\in\left\{0,1\right\}
:

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}_{b}\in E^{\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{b}}}

\displaystyle\implies\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in E^{\gamma(\pi)}

(42)

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}_{1-b}\in E^{\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{b}}}

\displaystyle\implies\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in E^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}

(43)

Proof of Claim
.

We use structural induction on
E
.

•

E=A\in\mathsf{BC}_{\mathcal{K}}
: By definition.

•

E=\exists R.B
:

(
42
):

Let
\delta^{\prime}_{b}\in(\exists R.B)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{b}})}
for some
b\in\left\{0,1\right\}
.
By definition of the semantics, there is an
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}\in\Delta^{\prime}
such that
\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{b},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{b}})}
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{b}})}
.
By definition of
R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{b}})}
the only possible reason for
\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{b},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{b}})}
is that

b=c
and
\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{b},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{diag}})}
, that is,

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{b}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi)}

Due to
b=c
, we can apply the induction hypothesis of (
42
) to
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{b}})}
and obtain

\forall\pi\in\mathsf{u}:\forall\delta\in\Delta:\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi)}

Togher with the above, we obtain

\forall\pi\in\sigma:\forall\delta\in\Delta:\left(\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{b}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi)}\text{ and }\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi)}\right)

Thus

\forall\pi\in\sigma:\forall\delta\in\Delta:\exists\varepsilon_{\pi,\delta}\in\Delta:\left(\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{b}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon_{\pi,\delta}\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi)}\text{ and }\varepsilon_{\pi,\delta}\in B^{\gamma(\pi)}\right)

namely we set
\varepsilon_{\pi,\delta}\mathrel{\,:=\,}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}(\pi,\delta)
in each case.
By the definition of the semantics we get

\forall\pi\in\sigma:\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in(\exists R.B)^{\gamma(\pi)}

(
43
):

Let
\delta^{\prime}_{1-b}\in(\exists R.B)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{b}})}
.
By the definition of the semantics, there exists some
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}\in\Delta^{\prime}
such that
\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{1-b},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{b}})}
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{b}})}
.
According to the definition of
R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{b}})}
, there are two possible cases:

(a)

c=1-b
and
\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{1-b}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}
.
We can apply the induction hypothesis of (
43
) to the fact that
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}=\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1-b}\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{b}})}
and obtain

\forall\pi\in\mathsf{u}:\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}

In combination with the presumption of this case, we obtain

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\left(\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{b}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}\text{ and }\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}\right)

thus proving the claim.

(b)

c=b
and there is some
\zeta^{\prime}_{b}\in\Delta^{\prime}
with
\zeta^{\prime}_{b}\approx\delta^{\prime}_{1-b}
and
\mbox{$\left\langle\zeta^{\prime}_{b},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathrm{diag}})}
.
The latter property implies

\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\mbox{$\left\langle\zeta^{\prime}_{b}(\pi_{*},\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}(\pi_{*},\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}

In turn, from
\zeta^{\prime}_{b}\approx\delta^{\prime}_{1-b}
we obtain

\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\bigcup_{\delta\in\Delta}\left\{\zeta^{\prime}_{b}(\pi_{*},\delta)\right\}=\mathop{\bigcup_{\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}),}}_{\delta\in\Delta}\left\{\delta^{\prime}_{1-b}(\pi,\delta)\right\}

This means in particular that

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\exists\delta_{+}\in\Delta:\zeta^{\prime}_{b}(\pi_{*},\delta_{+})=\delta^{\prime}_{1-b}(\pi,\delta)

whence

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\exists\delta_{+}\in\Delta:\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{1-b}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}(\pi_{*},\delta_{+})\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}

Now
b=c
means that we can apply the induction hypothesis of (
42
) to
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}=\varepsilon^{\prime}_{b}\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{b}})}
, from which we get

\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\varepsilon^{\prime}_{b}(\pi_{*},\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}

which we combine with the line above as usual to obtain

\forall\pi,\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\exists\delta_{+}\in\Delta:\left(\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{1-b}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}(\pi_{*},\delta_{+})\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}\text{ and }\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}(\pi_{*},\delta_{+})\in B^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}\right)

which in particular implies

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\exists\varepsilon\in\Delta:\left(\mbox{$\left\langle\delta^{\prime}_{1-b}(\pi,\delta),\varepsilon\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}\text{ and }\varepsilon\in B^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}\right)

namely in each case
\varepsilon\mathrel{\,:=\,}\varepsilon^{\prime}_{c}(\pi_{*},\delta_{+})
,
whence we get

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\delta^{\prime}_{1-b}(\pi,\delta)\in(\exists R.B)^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}

thus proving the claim.

•

E=\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B
:
Let
\pi^{\prime}\in\left\{\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}},\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}\right\}
and consider
\delta^{\prime}_{b}\in(\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.
Then there is some
\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}})
such that
\delta^{\prime}_{1-b}\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime\prime})}
.
By
5
we obtain

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}_{b}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime\prime}(\pi,\delta))}

whence for all
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in\Delta
we can set
\pi_{\pi,\delta}\mathrel{\,:=\,}\pi^{\prime\prime}(\pi,\delta)
to rewrite this into

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\exists\pi_{\pi,\delta}\in\sigma(\mathsf{s^{\prime}}):\delta^{\prime}_{b}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi_{\pi,\delta})}

and we conclude the claim:

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\delta^{\prime}_{b}(\pi,\delta)\in(\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B)^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}

•

E=\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B
:
Let
\pi^{\prime}\in\left\{\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}},\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}\right\}
and consider
\delta^{\prime}_{b}\in(\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}B)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.
Then for all
\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}})
, we have
\delta^{\prime}_{b}\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime\prime})}
, which by
5
means that

\forall\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}}):\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}_{b}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime\prime}(\pi,\delta))}

Let
\pi_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}\in\sigma(\mathsf{s^{\prime}})
be arbitrary.
Consider the function
\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}=\left\{(\pi,\delta)\mapsto\pi_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}\right\}
, for which we have
\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}})
by definition
and thus also

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\delta^{\prime}_{b}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}(\pi,\delta))}=B^{\gamma(\pi_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}})}

Since
\pi_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}
was arbitrarily chosen, we get

\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}\in\sigma(\mathsf{s^{\prime}}):\delta^{\prime}_{b}(\pi,\delta)\in B^{\gamma(\pi_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}})}

which shows the claim.

❑

We now continue the main proof, showing that
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
.

•

\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[C\sqsubseteq A]
: By definition.

•

\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{v}_{0}}}A\sqcap\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}}A\sqsubseteq D]
:
Let
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})
and
\delta^{\prime}_{b}\in(\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{v}_{0}}}A\sqcap\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}}A)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
for some
b\in\left\{0,1\right\}
.
We have to show
\delta^{\prime}_{b}\in D^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.
By definition of
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}
, we conclude that
\delta^{\prime}_{b}\in A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}})}\cap A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{1}})}
;
we furthermore have
\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}},\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{u})
and

A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{y}})}=C^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{y}})}
for all
y\in\left\{0,1\right\}
;
thus it follows that
\delta^{\prime}_{b}\in C^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}})}\cap C^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{1}})}
.
So in any case we obtain that
\delta^{\prime}_{b}\in C^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi_{\mathsf{v}_{1-b}})}
, whence by
6
we get
