\forall\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\forall\delta\in\Delta:\forall\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}):\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in C^{\gamma(\pi_{*})}

Hence, for all
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in\Delta
we have
\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in(\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}C)^{\gamma(\pi)}
.
From
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}C\sqsubseteq D]
it therefore follows that
\delta^{\prime}(\pi_{*},\delta)\in D^{\gamma(\pi)}
for all
\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
,
\delta\in\Delta
, and
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s})
.
Now by
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})
we obtain that for any
\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in\Delta
we get
\pi^{\prime}(\pi_{*},\delta)\in\sigma(\mathsf{s})
.
Thus it follows that
\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in D^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi_{*},\delta))}
for all
\pi,\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in\Delta
.
By
3
/
4
above, we get
\delta^{\prime}\in D^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.

•

\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\tau
for all axioms
\tau\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\setminus\left\{\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[C\sqsubseteq A],\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{v}_{0}}}A\sqcap\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}}A\sqsubseteq D]\right\}
:
We do a case distinction on the possible forms of axioms.

–

\tau=\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}[E\sqsubseteq F]
.
By presumption
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathcal{K}
we get that for every
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})
we have
E^{\gamma(\pi)}\subseteq F^{\gamma(\pi)}
.
Let
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})
and
\delta^{\prime}\in E^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.
From
5
we get that
\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in E^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}
for all
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in\Delta
.
From
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})
we get that
\pi^{\prime}(\pi_{*},\delta)\in\sigma(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})
for all
\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in\Delta
.
It follows that
E^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi_{*},\delta))}\subseteq F^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi_{*},\delta))}
for all
\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in\Delta
.
In particular,
\delta^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in F^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi_{*},\delta))}
for all
\pi,\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in\Delta
.
Therefore,
3
/
4
yields
\delta^{\prime}\in F^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.

–

\tau=\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}[R_{1}\circ\ldots\circ R_{n}\sqsubseteq R]
.
Let
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}})
and
\delta_{0}^{\prime},\delta_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\delta_{n}^{\prime}\in\Delta^{\prime}
with
\mbox{$\left\langle\delta_{i-1}^{\prime},\delta_{i}^{\prime}\right\rangle$}\in R_{i}^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
for all
1\leq i\leq n
.
Then by definition, for all
1\leq i\leq n
we get
\mbox{$\left\langle\delta_{i-1}^{\prime}(\pi,\delta),\delta_{i}^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R_{i}^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime})}
for all
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in\Delta
.
Since by presumption
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}[R_{1}\circ\ldots\circ R_{n}\sqsubseteq R]
, we get
\mbox{$\left\langle\delta_{0}^{\prime}(\pi,\delta),\delta_{n}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}
for all
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma(\mathsf{s^{\prime}})
,
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
, and
\delta\in\Delta
(since
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}})
implies that
\pi^{\prime}(\pi_{*},\delta)\in\sigma(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})
for all
\pi_{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in\Delta
).
Thus overall, we get
\mbox{$\left\langle\delta_{0}^{\prime},\delta_{n}^{\prime}\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
;
since
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}})
was arbitrary, we get
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models R_{1}\circ\ldots\circ R_{n}\sqsubseteq R
.

•

\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\alpha
for all assertions
\alpha\in\mathcal{K}
:
We do a case distinction on the possible forms of assertions.

–

\alpha=\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}[B(a)]
:
It follows that
B\in\mathsf{BC}_{\mathcal{K}}
since
\mathcal{K}
is in normal form.
From
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathcal{A}
we get that for any
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})
we have
a^{\gamma(\pi)}=\delta_{a}\in B^{\gamma(\pi)}
.
Let
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})
.
By definition, we get that
a^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}(\pi,\delta)=\delta_{a}
for all
\delta\in\Delta
and all
\pi\in\Pi
and in particular for all
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
.
From
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})
we get that for any
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in\Delta
we find
\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in\sigma(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})
.
It thus follows that
a^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}(\pi,\delta)=\delta_{a}\in B^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}
for all
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in\Delta
.
Consequently,
a^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}\in B^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.

–

\alpha=\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}[R(a,c)]
:
From
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathcal{A}
it follows that for every
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})
we have
\mbox{$\left\langle a^{\gamma(\pi)},c^{\gamma(\pi)}\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi)}
.
Let
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})
.
As above, we get
a^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}(\pi,\delta)=\delta_{a}
and
c^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}(\pi,\delta)=\delta_{c}
for every
\delta\in\Delta
and
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
.
From
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})
, we get
\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in\sigma(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})
for every
\delta\in\Delta
and
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
.
Thus
\mbox{$\left\langle a^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}(\pi,\delta),a^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}(\pi,\delta)\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta))}
for all
\delta\in\Delta
and
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
whence

\mbox{$\left\langle a^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})},a^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}\right\rangle$}\in R^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.

•

\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\zeta
for all sharpening statements
\zeta\in\mathcal{K}
:
Let
\mathsf{s}_{1}\cap\ldots\cap\mathsf{s}_{n}\preceq\mathsf{s}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}
and
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s}_{1})\cap\ldots\cap\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s}_{n})
.
Then by definition of
\sigma^{\prime}
, we get
\mathop{\bigcup_{\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u}),}}_{\delta\in\Delta}\left\{\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\right\}\subseteq\sigma(\mathsf{s}_{i})
for all
1\leq i\leq n
.
Since
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathsf{s}_{1}\cap\ldots\cap\mathsf{s}_{n}\preceq\mathsf{s^{\prime}}
, we get that for all
\pi\in\sigma(\mathsf{u})
and
\delta\in\Delta
we have
\pi^{\prime}(\pi,\delta)\in\sigma(\mathsf{s^{\prime}})
.
By definition of
\sigma^{\prime}
this means that
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s^{\prime}})
.

(b)
Let
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
and consider
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{s})
and
\delta^{\prime}\in(\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}C)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.
Since
\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}},\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}\in\sigma^{\prime}(\mathsf{u})
, we thus get
\delta^{\prime}\in C^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}})}\cap C^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{1}})}
.
By
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[C\sqsubseteq A]
, we obtain that
\delta^{\prime}\in A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{0}})}\cap A^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{\mathsf{v}_{1}})}
;
that is,
\delta^{\prime}\in(\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{v}_{0}}}A\sqcap\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}}A)^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.
Since also
\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{v}_{0}}}A\sqcap\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}}A\sqsubseteq D]
, it follows that
\delta^{\prime}\in D^{\gamma^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})}
.

Appendix B
Proofs for
Section

3

Theorem 6
(Soundness).

If a
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{E\!L}+}
knowledge base
\mathcal{K}
is satisfiable, there is a non-contradictory calculus derivation for knowledge base
\mathcal{K}
.

Proof.

Suppose
\mathcal{K}
is satisfiable. Then there is a model
\mathfrak{D}=\mbox{$\left\langle\Delta,\Pi,\sigma,\gamma\right\rangle$}
such that
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathcal{K}
.

To show soundness it suffices to show that for each rule
\Gamma|\theta
, we have
\mathfrak{D}\models\theta
if
\mathfrak{D}\models\Gamma
. This can be seen easily for both axioms and rules, and hence limit ourselves to show some examples.

•

Axions

(T.1)

By the definition of
\sigma
, for all
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\in\mathsf{ST}_{\mathcal{K}}
we have
\sigma(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})\subseteq\sigma(\hbox{$*$})
. Hence,
\mathfrak{D}\models\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\preceq\hbox{$*$}
as desired.

(T.3)

Let
\pi\in\Pi
be a precisification and let
\delta\in\Delta
be a domain element.
Since
B\Rightarrow C\equiv\neg B\sqcup C
,
if
\delta\in C^{\gamma(\pi)}
then clearly

\delta\in(C\Rightarrow C)^{\gamma(\pi)}
.
Similarly, if
\delta\notin C^{\gamma(\pi)}

then also
\delta\in(C\Rightarrow C)^{\gamma(\pi)}
.
Thus clearly
(C\Rightarrow C)^{\gamma(\pi)}=\Delta
,
and hence
\mathfrak{D}\models\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[C\Rightarrow C]
as desired.

•

Rules

(S.1)

Assume that
\mathfrak{D}\models{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}}
and
\mathfrak{D}\models\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime\prime}}$}
. Then we have that
\sigma(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})\subseteq\sigma(\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$})
and
\sigma(\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$})\subseteq\sigma(\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime\prime}}$})
, thus
\sigma(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})\subseteq\sigma(\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime\prime}}$})
and consequently
\mathfrak{D}\models{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime\prime}}$}}
as desired.

(F.4)

Assume that
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{t}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}D]
and
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}[D\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}E]
. If
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{t}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}D]
, then for all
\pi\in\sigma(\mathrm{t})
if
\delta\in C^{\gamma(\pi)}
then there is some
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}^{\prime})
such that
\delta\in D^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime})}
. Moreover, since
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}[D\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}E]
and
\delta\in D^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime})}
, there is some
\pi^{\prime\prime}\in\sigma(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
such that
\delta\in E^{\gamma(\pi^{\prime\prime})}
, and thus
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{t}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}E]
as desired.

Theorem 7
(Completeness).

If there is a non-contradictory calculus derivation for a
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{E\!L}+}
knowledge base
\mathcal{K}
, then
\mathcal{K}
is satisfiable.

Model construction

Given a Standpoint
\mathcal{E\!L}
knowledge base
\mathcal{K}
in normal form, let
\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
denote the set of axioms obtained by saturating
\mathcal{K}
under the above deduction rules.
Assuming
\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
does not contain
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\Rightarrow\bot]]
, we construct a model of
\mathcal{K}
in an infinite process: we start from an initialized model and extend it (both by adding domain elements and precisifications) in a stepwise fashion, resulting in a “monotonic” sequence of models. The result of the process is the “limit” of this sequence, which can be expressed via an infinite union.

For the initialization, we choose the standpoint structure

\mathfrak{D}_{0}=\mbox{$\left\langle\Delta_{0},\Pi_{0},\sigma_{0},\gamma_{0}\right\rangle$}
where:

•

\Delta_{0}
consists of one element
\delta_{a}
for every individual name
a
mentioned in
\mathcal{K}
;

•

\Pi_{0}
consists of one precisification
\pi_{\mathsf{s}}
for every standpoint
\mathsf{s}
mentioned in
\mathcal{K}
(including
*
);

•

\sigma_{0}
maps each standpoint
\mathsf{s}
to the set
\{\pi_{\mathsf{s}}\}\cup\{\pi_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}\mid\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}\prec\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}\}
;

•

\gamma_{0}
maps each
\pi_{\mathsf{s}}
to the description logic interpretation
\mathcal{I}
over
\Delta_{0}
, where

–

a^{\mathcal{I}}=\delta_{a}
for each individual name
a
.

–

A^{\mathcal{I}}=\{\delta_{a}\mid\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[\{a\}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\top\Rightarrow A]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash},\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\in\sigma^{-1}_{0}(\pi_{\mathsf{s}})\}

–

R^{\mathcal{I}}=\{(\delta_{a},\delta_{b})\mid\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[R(a,b)]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash},\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\in\sigma^{-1}_{0}(\pi_{\mathsf{s}})\}

for each role name
R
;

Note that the obtained structure satisfies all axioms of
\mathcal{K}
except for those of the shape
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[E\sqsubseteq\exists R.F]
.
This will also be the case for all structures
\mathfrak{D}_{1}
,
\mathfrak{D}_{2}
, … produced in the following.

Moreover, we define
\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta_{a})=\{C\mid\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[\{a\}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\top\Rightarrow C]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash},\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\in\sigma^{-1}_{0}(\pi)\}
.

Given such a structure
\mathfrak{D}_{i}=\mbox{$\left\langle\Delta_{i},\Pi_{i},\sigma_{i},\gamma_{i}\right\rangle$}
, check if it satisfies all axioms of the form
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{t}}}[E\sqsubseteq\exists R.F]
. If so,
\mathfrak{D}_{i}
is a model of
\mathcal{K}
and we are done. Otherwise, we pick some
\delta^{*}\in\Delta_{i}
and some
\pi^{*}\in\Pi_{i}
with
\pi^{*}\in\sigma(\mathsf{t})
for which
\delta^{*}\in E^{\gamma(\pi^{*})}\setminus(\exists R.F)^{\gamma(\pi^{*})}
for some (previously unsatisfied) axiom
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{t}}}[E\sqsubseteq\exists R.F]
from
\mathcal{K}
.
Among the admissible pairs
\delta^{*}
,
\pi^{*}
, we pick one that is minimal wrt.
\min\{j\leq i\mid\delta^{*}\in\Delta_{j}\}+\min\{j\leq i\mid\pi^{*}\in\Pi_{j}\}
.

Then obtain
\mathfrak{D}_{i+1}=\mbox{$\left\langle\Delta_{i+1},\Pi_{i+1},\sigma_{i+1},\gamma_{i+1}\right\rangle$}
from

\mathfrak{D}_{i}=\mbox{$\left\langle\Delta_{i},\Pi_{i},\sigma_{i},\gamma_{i}\right\rangle$}
as follows:

•

\Delta_{i+1}=\Delta_{i}\cup\{\delta^{\prime}\}
, where
\delta^{\prime}
is a fresh domain element;

•

let
\mathrm{Con}(F,\pi^{*})=\{A\mid\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[F\Rightarrow A]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash},\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\in\sigma^{-1}(\pi^{*})\}

•

\Pi_{i+1}
is obtained from
\Pi_{i}
by adding a fresh precisification
\pi_{\delta^{\prime},\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}D}

whenever there is some
C\in\mathrm{Con}(F,\pi^{*})
with
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{t}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}D]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
for some
\mathsf{t}\in\sigma^{-1}(\pi^{*})

•

let
\sigma_{i+1}
be such that
\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime\prime}}$})=\sigma_{i}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime\prime}}$})\cup\left\{\pi_{\delta^{\prime},\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}D}\right\}
if
\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime\prime}}$}\in\{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\}\cup\{\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}\mid\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\prec\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}\}
and
\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime\prime}}$})=\sigma_{i}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime\prime}}$})
otherwise.

•

for
\pi\in\Pi_{i+1}
we let

\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta^{\prime}):=\begin{cases}\mathrm{Con}(F,\pi^{*})&\text{ if }\pi=\pi^{*}\\
\displaystyle\bigcup_{{G\in\mathrm{Con}(F,\pi^{*})},\ {\mathsf{s}\in\sigma^{-1}_{i+1}(\pi)},\ {\mathsf{t}\in\sigma^{-1}_{i+1}(\pi^{*})}}\{A\mid\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{t}}}[G\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\top\Rightarrow A]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}\}&\text{ if }\pi\in\Pi_{i}\setminus\{\pi^{*}\}\\
\displaystyle\bigcup_{{G\in\mathrm{Con}(F,\pi^{*})},\ {\mathsf{s}\in\sigma^{-1}_{i+1}(\pi)},\ {\mathsf{t}\in\sigma^{-1}_{i+1}(\pi^{*})}}\{A\mid\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{t}}}[G\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[D\Rightarrow A]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}\}&\text{ if }\pi=\pi_{\delta^{\prime},\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}D}\in\Pi_{i+1}\setminus\Pi_{i}\\
\end{cases}

•

Let
\gamma_{i+1}
be the interpretation function defined as follows:

–

a^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}=a^{\gamma_{i}(\pi)}=\delta_{a}
for each individual name
a
and each
\pi\in\Pi_{i+1}
.

–

for concept names
A
, we let
A^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}=A^{\gamma_{i}(\pi)}\cup\{\delta^{\prime}\}
if
A\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta^{\prime})
, and
A^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}=A^{\gamma_{i}(\pi)}
otherwise.

–

for all role names
T
, we obtain
T^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}
through a concurrent saturation process, that is,

T^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}=\bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}[T^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{k}
, where we let

[T^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{0}=\mathit{Self}\cup\mathit{Other}
, with

\displaystyle\mathit{Self}=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\{(\delta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})\}&\text{ if }\exists T.\mathsf{Self}\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta^{\prime}),\\
\emptyset&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathit{Other}=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\emptyset&\text{ whenever }\pi\in\Pi_{i+1}\setminus\Pi_{i}\\
T^{\gamma_{i}(\pi)}&\text{ for }\pi\in\Pi_{i}\text{ whenever }\ T\neq R\text{ or }\pi\neq\pi^{*}\\
R^{\gamma_{i}(\pi^{*})}\cup(\delta^{*},\delta^{\prime})&\text{ if }T=R\text{ and }\pi=\pi^{*}\end{cases}

Moreover, obtain

[T^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{k+1}=[T^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{k}\cup\bigcup_{{\mathsf{s}\in\sigma^{-1}_{i+1}(\pi)}\atop{\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[R_{0}\sqsubseteq T]\in\mathcal{K}^{\vdash}}}[R_{0}^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{k}\cup\bigcup_{{\mathsf{s}\in\sigma^{-1}_{i+1}(\pi)}\atop{\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[R_{1}\circ R_{2}\sqsubseteq T]\in\mathcal{K}^{\vdash}}}[R_{1}^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{k}\circ[R_{2}^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{k}

After producing the (potentially infinite) sequence
\mathfrak{D}_{0},\mathfrak{D}_{1},\ldots
we obtain the wanted model
\mathfrak{D}
via

\mathfrak{D}=\mbox{$\left\langle\bigcup_{i}\Delta_{i},\bigcup_{i}\Pi_{i},\bigcup_{i}\sigma_{i},\bigcup_{i}\gamma_{i}\right\rangle$}

Lemma 8
.

Let
\mathfrak{D}_{i}
be as described by the model construction, and let
\pi\in\Pi_{i}
. Then, there is a standpoint
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}\in\sigma^{-1}_{i}(\pi)
such that for all
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\in\sigma^{-1}_{i}(\pi)
we have
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}\prec\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
.

Proof.

This follows easily by the construction of
\sigma
.
First, consider the case of
\mathfrak{D}_{0}
. By definition,
\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})=\{\pi_{\mathsf{s}}\}\cup\{\pi_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}\mid\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}\prec\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}\}
, and since by
T.2
we have
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\prec\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, hence the claim holds for
\mathfrak{D}_{0}
. Now we assume that the claim holds for
\mathfrak{D}_{i}
and we show that it also holds for
\mathfrak{D}_{i+1}
. Recall that
\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})=\sigma_{i}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})\cup\left\{\pi_{\delta^{\prime},\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime\prime}}}}D}\right\}
if
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\in\{\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime\prime}}$}\}\cup\{\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}\mid\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\prec\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}\}
and
\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})=\sigma_{i}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
otherwise. Again by
T.2
we have
\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime\prime}}$}\prec\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime\prime}}$}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, hence the claim holds for
\mathfrak{D}_{i+1}
and thus for
\mathfrak{D}
as desired.

Lemma 9
.

Let
\mathfrak{D}_{i}
be as described by the model construction, and let
\pi\in\Pi_{i}
. Then, if
\phi\in\mathcal{K}^{\vdash}
,
\pi\in\sigma_{i}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
and
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}
is a symbol in
\phi
, then the formula
\phi^{\pi}
replacing some occurrences of
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}
by
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}
is also in the saturated set,
\phi^{\pi}\in\mathcal{K}^{\vdash}
.

Proof.

By
Lemma

8
and
\pi\in\sigma_{i}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
, we have that
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}\prec\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
. Then it is easy to see that for
\phi
having any formula shape with a
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}
, we can apply rule S.3 and rule S.4 and obtain precisely that each formula
\phi^{\pi}\in\mathcal{K}^{\vdash}
, with
\phi^{\pi}
being any formula resulting from replacing one or more occurrences of
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}
by
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}
.

Lemma 10
.

Let
\mathfrak{D}_{i}
be as described by the model construction. Then
A\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
iff
\delta\in A^{\gamma_{i}(\pi)}
and
\,\exists R.\mathsf{Self}\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
iff
(\delta,\delta)\in R^{\gamma_{i}(\pi)}
.

Proof.

For the concept names the case is trivial since the definition coincides. Thus, we show the case of self-loops by induction on the model construction.

Base Case

: Consider
\exists R.\mathsf{Self}\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
of
\mathfrak{D}_{0}
.
(\Rightarrow)
If
\exists R.\mathsf{Self}\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
then
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[\{a\}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\top\Rightarrow\exists R.\mathsf{Self}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
for
\pi\in\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
. Then we obtain
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[R(a,a)]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by rule L.1, and thus by construction
(\delta,\delta)\in R^{\gamma_{0}(\pi)}
.
(\Leftarrow)
If
(\delta,\delta)\in R^{\gamma_{0}(\pi)}
then by construction we have some
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[R(a,a)]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
with
\pi\in\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
. Then by the rule L.4 we obtain
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[\{a\}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\top\Rightarrow\exists R.\mathsf{Self}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
and hence
\exists R.\mathsf{Self}\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
as desired.

Inductive Step

: By the inductive hypothesis, assume that the statement holds for
\mathfrak{D}_{i}
and we show that it also holds for
\mathfrak{D}_{i+1}
.

Consider
\exists R.\mathsf{Self}\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta^{\prime})
of
\mathfrak{D}_{i+1}
.
(\Rightarrow)
If
\exists R.\mathsf{Self}\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta^{\prime})
then by construction
(\delta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})\in R^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}
, and we notice that for
\delta\in\Delta_{i}\setminus\Delta_{i+1}
the same holds by induction.
(\Leftarrow)
This is the harder case. We show that if
(\delta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})\in R^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}
then
\exists R.\mathsf{Self}\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta^{\prime})
. We observe the saturation process that defines
R^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}
and we notice that for every pair
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})\in[R^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{k+1}
and
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})\notin[R^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{k}
, we have
\delta^{\prime}=\delta_{2}
. With regards to self-loops, we will ext show that, since
\delta^{\prime}
is fresh, relations of the shape
(\delta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})\in[R^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{k+1}
can exclusively be triggered by relations
(\delta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})\in[R^{\prime\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{0}
, i.e. elements in the set
\mathit{Self}
.

•

By definition if
(\delta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})\in[R^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{0}
then necessarily
\exists R.\mathsf{Self}\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta^{\prime})
since
\delta^{\prime}
is fresh.

•

Let
(\delta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})\notin[R^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{k}
and
(\delta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})\in[R^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{k+1}
. Then either

Case 1

(\delta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})\in\{[R_{0}^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{k}\mid\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}[R_{0}\sqsubseteq R]\in\mathcal{K}^{\vdash},\ \hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}\in\sigma^{-1}_{i+1}(\pi)\}
Thus, by
Lemma

9
, there must be some
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[R_{0}\sqsubseteq R]\in\mathcal{K}^{\vdash}
such that
(\delta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})\in[R_{0}^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{k}
.

Case 1.1

\pi=\pi^{*}
and

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[F\Rightarrow\exists R_{0}.\mathsf{Self}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by the assumption that
\exists R_{0}.\mathsf{Self}\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta^{\prime})

(2)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[R_{0}\sqsubseteq R]\in\mathcal{K}^{\vdash}
by the assumption

(3)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\exists R_{0}.\mathsf{Self}\Rightarrow\exists R.\mathsf{Self}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by (2) and rule L.5

(4)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[F\Rightarrow\exists R.\mathsf{Self}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by (1), (3) and rule C.1, thus
\exists R.\mathsf{Self}\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta^{\prime})
as desired

Completeness proof.

In order to show completeness, we will show that if the model construction fails, then necessarily the saturated knowledge base contains the innconsistency axiom,
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\Rightarrow\bot]]\in\mathcal{K}^{\vdash}
. First, we will use the previously constructed model to show that for all
\phi\in\mathcal{K}
then
\mathfrak{D}\models\phi
. This is proved by induction for all types of axioms allowed in the normal form. By
Lemma

10
, we use
C\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
in place of
\delta\in C^{\gamma_{i}(\pi)}
for
C
a concept name and of
(\delta,\delta)\in R^{\gamma_{i}(\pi)}
when
C
is of the form
\exists R.\mathsf{Self}
.

Claim 7
.

Let
\mathcal{K}
be a
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{E\!L}+}
knowledge base and let
\mathfrak{D}
be the model obtained by the model construction. Then for all
\phi\in\mathcal{K}
we have
\mathfrak{D}\models\phi
.

\phi=\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}

Assume
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}\in\mathcal{K}
. Then it is clear from the definition of
\sigma_{0}
that by construction
\mathfrak{D}_{0}\models\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}
. Now, we assume that
\mathfrak{D}_{i}\models\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}
and show that
\mathfrak{D}_{i+1}\models\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}
.

1.

Notice that we have
\sigma_{i}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})\subseteq\sigma_{i}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$})
and
\sigma_{i+1}
is such that for all
\pi\in\Pi_{i}
and standpoint
\mathsf{s^{\prime\prime}}
,
\pi\in\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime\prime}}$})
if
\pi\in\sigma_{i}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime\prime}}$})
.

2.

So we must only show that if
\pi\in\Pi_{i+1}\setminus\Pi_{i}
and
\pi\in\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
then we must have
\pi\in\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$})
.

3.

By the construction of
\sigma_{i+1}
, if
\pi\in\Pi_{i+1}\setminus\Pi_{i}
, then there is a standpoint
\mathsf{s^{\prime\prime}}
for which
\pi
has been created such that either
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}=\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime\prime}}$}
or
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\prec\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime\prime}}$}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
.

4.

If
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}=\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime\prime}}$}
, then
\pi\in\sigma_{i+1}(s)
as desired, from the definition of
\sigma_{i+1}
and from
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
.

5.

Otherwise, since
\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
is saturated under the deduction rules and by the assumption, we have
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, then by the non-applicability of the rule S.1, we must also have
\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime\prime}}$}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, which again by the definition of
\sigma_{i+1}
implies that
\pi\in\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$})
as desired.

6.

Thus, necessarily
\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})\subseteq\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$})
and
\mathfrak{D}_{i+1}\models\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}
.

7.

Finally, from the definition of
\mathfrak{D}
we obtain
\mathfrak{D}\models\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}
as desired.

\phi=\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1}\cap\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}

Assume
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1}\cap\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}\in\mathcal{K}
. Let us first show that
\mathfrak{D}_{0}\models\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1}\cap\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}
.

Case 1:

Assume that
\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1})\cap\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2})=\emptyset
. Then by construction there is no
\mathsf{s}
such that
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\prec\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
and
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\prec\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, and
\mathfrak{D}_{0}\models\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1}\cap\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}
is trivially satisfied.

Case 2:

Assume that
\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1})\cap\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2})\neq\emptyset
. Then, by the construction of
\sigma_{0}
, if
\pi\in\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1})\cap\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2})
then there must be some
\mathsf{s}
such that
\pi\in\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
and both
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\prec\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
and
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\prec\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, thus
\pi\in\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1})
and
\pi\in\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2})
.
Then, by the non-applicability of the rules S.1 and S.2, also
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\prec\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, and thus also
\pi\in\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$})
. Hence,
\mathfrak{D}_{0}\models\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1}\cap\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}
as desired.

We now assume that
\mathfrak{D}_{i}\models\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1}\cap\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}
and, in a similar way, we show that then
\mathfrak{D}_{i+1}\models\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1}\cap\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}
.

Case 1:

Assume that
\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1})\cap\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2})=\emptyset
. Then by construction there is no
\mathsf{s}
such that
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\prec\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
and
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\prec\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, and
\mathfrak{D}_{i+1}\models\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1}\cap\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}
is trivially satisfied.

Case 2:

Assume that
\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1})\cap\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2})\neq\emptyset
. Then, by the construction of
\sigma_{i+1}
, if
\pi\in\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1})\cap\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2})
then either (a)
\pi\in\Pi_{i+1}\cap\Pi_{i}
and thus
\pi\in\sigma_{i}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$})
by the inductive hypothesis, giving us
\pi\in\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$})
as required or (b)
\pi\in\Pi_{i+1}\setminus\Pi_{i}
, and hence there must be some
\mathsf{s}
such that
\pi\in\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
and both
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\prec\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
and
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\prec\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, thus
\pi\in\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1})
and
\pi\in\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2})
. Then, again by the non-applicability of the rules S.1 and S.2, also
\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}\prec\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, and thus also
\pi\in\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$})
. Hence,
\mathfrak{D}_{i+1}\models\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1}\cap\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}
as desired.

Finally, from the definition of
\mathfrak{D}
we obtain
\mathfrak{D}\models\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{1}\cap\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$}_{2}\preceq\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$}
as desired.

\phi=\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}{[R\sqsubseteq R^{\prime}]}

Assume
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[R\sqsubseteq R^{\prime}]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
.

Base Case

: First, let us show that
\mathfrak{D}_{0}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[R\sqsubseteq R^{\prime}]
. We let
\pi\in\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
and
(\delta,\delta^{\prime})\in R^{\gamma_{0}(\pi)}
.

(1)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[R(a,b)]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by construction and
Lemma

9

(2)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[R\sqsubseteq R^{\prime}]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by assumption and
Lemma

9

(3)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[R^{\prime}(a,b)]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by (1), (2) and rule A.4

From this we obtain that if
\pi\in\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
and
(\delta,\delta^{\prime})\in R^{\gamma_{0}(\pi)}
then
(\delta,\delta^{\prime})\in R^{\prime\gamma_{0}(\pi)}
, thus
\mathfrak{D}_{0},\pi\models R\sqsubseteq R^{\prime}
for all
\pi\in\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
and hence
\mathfrak{D}_{0}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[R\sqsubseteq R^{\prime}]
by the semantics.

Inductive Step

: Now, we assume that
\mathfrak{D}_{i}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[R\sqsubseteq R^{\prime}]
and show that
\mathfrak{D}_{i+1}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[R\sqsubseteq R^{\prime}]
.
We must show that if
(\delta,\delta^{\prime})\in R^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}
then
(\delta,\delta^{\prime})\in R^{\prime\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}
for all
\pi\in\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
.
From the concurrent saturation process that determines
R^{\prime\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}
, we precisely have that
(\delta,\delta^{\prime})\in[R^{\prime\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{k+1}
if
(\delta,\delta^{\prime})\in[R^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{k}
,
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[R\sqsubseteq R^{\prime}]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
and
\pi\in\sigma(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
, and hence it easily follows that
\mathfrak{D}_{i+1}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[R\sqsubseteq R^{\prime}]
as desired.

\phi=\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}{[R_{1}\circ R_{2}\sqsubseteq R^{\prime}]}

Assume
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[R_{1}\circ R_{2}\sqsubseteq R^{\prime}]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}
.

Base Case

: First, let us show that
\mathfrak{D}_{0}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[R_{1}\circ R_{2}\sqsubseteq R^{\prime}]
. We let
\pi\in\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
,
(\delta,\delta^{\prime})\in R_{1}^{\gamma_{0}(\pi)}
and
(\delta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime\prime})\in R_{2}^{\gamma_{0}(\pi)}
.

(1)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}(R_{1}\circ R_{2}\sqsubseteq R^{\prime})\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
and
\pi\in\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
by assumption and
Lemma

9

(2)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[R(a,b)]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by construction and
Lemma

9

(3)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[R(b,c)]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by construction and
Lemma

9

(4)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[R^{\prime}(a,c)]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by (1), (2), (3) and rule A.5

From this we obtain that if
\pi\in\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
,
(\delta,\delta^{\prime})\in R_{1}^{\gamma_{0}(\pi)}
and
(\delta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime\prime})\in R_{2}^{\gamma_{0}(\pi)}
then
(\delta,\delta^{\prime\prime})\in R^{\prime\gamma_{0}(\pi)}
. Thus
\mathfrak{D}_{0},\pi\models R_{1}\circ R_{2}\sqsubseteq R^{\prime}
for all
\pi\in\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
and hence
\mathfrak{D}_{0}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[R_{1}\circ R_{2}\sqsubseteq R^{\prime}]
by the semantics.

Inductive Step

: Now, we assume that
\mathfrak{D}_{i}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}(R_{1}\circ R_{2}\sqsubseteq R^{\prime})
and show that
\mathfrak{D}_{i+1}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[R_{1}\circ R_{2}\sqsubseteq R^{\prime}]
.
We must show that if
(\delta,\delta^{\prime})\in R_{1}^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}
and
(\delta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime\prime})\in R_{2}^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}
then
(\delta,\delta^{\prime\prime})\in R^{\prime\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}
for all
\pi\in\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
.
From the concurrent saturation process that determines
R_{1}^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)},R_{2}^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}
and
R^{\prime\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}
and the assumption, we have that for some
k<m

(\delta,\delta^{\prime})\in[R_{1}^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{k}
and for some
l<m

(\delta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime\prime})\in[R_{2}^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{l}
, thus also
(\delta,\delta^{\prime})\in[R_{1}^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{m-1}
and
(\delta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime\prime})\in[R_{2}^{\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{m-1}
. Hence we also have
(\delta,\delta^{\prime\prime})\in[R^{\prime\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}]_{m}
and finally
(\delta,\delta^{\prime\prime})\in R^{\prime\gamma_{i+1}(\pi)}
as desired.

\phi=\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}{[C\sqsubseteq D]}

Assume
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[C\sqsubseteq D]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}
(i.e.
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[C\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
and thus also
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[C\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
due to rule I.2).

Base Case

: First, let us show that
\mathfrak{D}_{0}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[C\sqsubseteq D]
. We let
\pi\in\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
and
C\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
.

(1)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[C\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by assumption

(2)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[\{a\}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow C]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by construction,
Lemma

9
and the assumption that
C\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)

(3)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[C\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by (1) and
Lemma

9

(4)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\{a\}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[C\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by (3) and rule A.1

(5)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[\{a\}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[C\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by (4) and rule S.4

(6)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[\{a\}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by (2), (5) rule C.1, and thus
D\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
as required

From this we obtain that if
\pi\in\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
and
C\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
then
D\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
, thus
\mathfrak{D}_{0},\pi\models C\sqsubseteq D
for all
\pi\in\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
and hence
\mathfrak{D}_{0}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[C\sqsubseteq D]
by the semantics.

Inductive Step

: Now, we assume that
\mathfrak{D}_{i}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[C\sqsubseteq D]
and show that
\mathfrak{D}_{i+1}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[C\sqsubseteq D]
. Consider
\delta\in\Delta_{i+1}\setminus\Delta_{i}
(the case of
\delta\in\Delta_{i}
is trivial). Thus,
\delta
has been introduced to satisfy some axiom
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{t}}}[E\sqsubseteq\exists R.F]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
with
\hbox{$\mathsf{t}$}\in\sigma_{i+1}(\pi^{*})
. We must show that if
C\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
then
D\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
for all
\pi\in\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
. We assume
C\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
and consider the three cases.

Case 1

: Assume
\pi=\pi^{*}
and
\pi\in\sigma(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
. Since
C\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
and by rule I.2 and
Lemma

9
, we have
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[F\Rightarrow C]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
. By assumption
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[C\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
. Then by rule C.1 we obtain
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[F\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, and thus
D\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta^{\prime})
as required.

Case 2

: Assume
\pi\in\Pi_{i}\setminus\{\pi^{*}\}
and
\pi\in\sigma(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
. Since
C\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta^{\prime})
then for some
{G\in\mathrm{Con}(F,\pi^{*})}
there is
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[G\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow C]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by
Lemma

9
. By assumption,
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[C\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
.

(1)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[C\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by assumption

(2)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[G\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow C]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by assumption

(3)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[C\Rightarrow\top]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by axiom T.4 and rule S.3

(4)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[C\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by (1), (3) and axiom C.2

(5)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[G\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[C\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by (2), (4) and axiom F.1

(6)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[G\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by (2), (5) and axiom C.1. Thus we obtain
D\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
as desired

Case 3

: Assume
\pi=\pi_{\delta^{\prime},\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}E}
and
\pi\in\sigma(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
. Since
C\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta^{\prime})

then for some
{G\in\mathrm{Con}(F,\pi^{*})}
by
Lemma

9
there is

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[G\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[E\Rightarrow C]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
. Recall that, by assumption,

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[C\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
.

(1)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[C\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by assumption

(2)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[G\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[E\Rightarrow C]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by assumption

(3)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[G\Rightarrow\top]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by axiom T.4 and S.4

(4)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[G\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[C\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by (1), (3) and axiom C.2

(5)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[G\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[E\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by (2), (4) and axiom C.1. Thus we obtain
D\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
as desired

\phi=\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}{[C_{1}\sqcap C_{2}\sqsubseteq D]}

Let us now show that if
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[C_{1}\sqcap C_{2}\sqsubseteq D]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}
then
\mathfrak{D}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[C_{1}\sqcap C_{2}\sqsubseteq D]
.

Base Case

: First, let us show that
\mathfrak{D}_{0}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[C_{1}\sqcap C_{2}\sqsubseteq D]
. Let
\pi\in\Pi
,
C_{1}\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
,
C_{2}\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
and
\delta=a^{\gamma_{0}}
. We need to show that
D\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)

(1)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\{a\}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow C_{1}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by construction, rule A.3, and
Lemma

9

(2)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\{a\}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow C_{2}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by construction, rule A.3, and
Lemma

9

(3)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[C_{1}\sqcap C_{2}\sqsubseteq D]
by assumption and
Lemma

9

(4)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\{a\}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by (1), (2), (3) and rule E.4, thus we obtain
D\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
as desired

Inductive Step

: Now, we assume that
\mathfrak{D}_{i}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[C_{1}\sqcap C_{2}\sqsubseteq D]
and show that
\mathfrak{D}_{i+1}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[C_{1}\sqcap C_{2}\sqsubseteq D]
. Consider
\delta\in\Delta_{i+1}\setminus\Delta_{i}
(the case of
\delta\in\Delta_{i}
is trivial). Thus,
\delta
has been introduced to satisfy some axiom
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{t}}}[E\sqsubseteq\exists R.F]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
with
\hbox{$\mathsf{t}$}\in\sigma_{i+1}(\pi^{*})
. We must show that if
C_{1}\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
and
C_{2}\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
then
D\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
for all
\pi\in\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
. We assume
C_{1},C_{2}\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
and consider the three cases.

Case 1

: Assume
\pi=\pi^{*}
and
\pi\in\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
. Since
C_{1},C_{2}\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
then (by construction and rule A.3)
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[F\Rightarrow C_{1}]],\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[F\Rightarrow C_{2}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by
Lemma

9
and also
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[C_{1}\sqcap C_{2}\sqsubseteq D]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
. Then by rule E.4 we obtain
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[F\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, and thus
D\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta^{\prime})
as required.

Case 2

: Assume
\pi\in\Pi_{i}\setminus\{\pi^{*}\}
and
\pi\in\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
. Since
C_{1},C_{2}\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta^{\prime})
then for some
{G_{1},G_{2}\in\mathrm{Con}(F,\pi^{*})}
and by
Lemma

9
, there are
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[G_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow C_{1}]],\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[G_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow C_{2}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
:

(1)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[C_{1}\sqcap C_{2}\sqsubseteq D]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
,

(2)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[G_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow C_{1}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}

(3)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[G_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow C_{2}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}

(4)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u_{1}}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[F\Rightarrow G_{1}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by the construction of
\mathrm{Con}(F,\pi^{*})
and
Lemma

9

(5)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u_{2}}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[F\Rightarrow G_{2}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by the construction of
\mathrm{Con}(F,\pi^{*})
and
Lemma

9

(6)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[F\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow C_{1}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by (2), (4) and rule C.2

(7)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[F\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow C_{2}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by (3), (5) and rule C.2

(8)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[F\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by (1), (6), (7) and rule E.4, thus
D\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta^{\prime})
as desired

Case 3

: Assume
\pi=\pi_{\delta^{\prime},\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}E}\in\Pi_{i+1}\setminus\Pi_{i}
and
\pi\in\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
. Since
C_{1},C_{2}\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta^{\prime})
then for some
{G_{1},G_{2}\in\mathrm{Con}(F,\pi^{*})}
and by
Lemma

9
, there are
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[G_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[E\Rightarrow C_{1}]],\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[G_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[E\Rightarrow C_{2}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}

(1)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[C_{1}\sqcap C_{2}\sqsubseteq D]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
,

(2)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[G_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[E\Rightarrow C_{1}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}

(3)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[G_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[E\Rightarrow C_{2}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}

(4)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u_{1}}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[F\Rightarrow G_{1}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by the construction of
\mathrm{Con}(F,\pi^{*})
and
Lemma

9

(5)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u_{2}}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[F\Rightarrow G_{2}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by the construction of
\mathrm{Con}(F,\pi^{*})
and
Lemma

9

(6)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[F\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[E\Rightarrow C_{1}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by (2), (4) and rule C.2

(7)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[F\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[E\Rightarrow C_{2}]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by (3), (5) and rule C.2

(8)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[F\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[E\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by (6), (7) and rule E.4, thus
D\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta^{\prime})
as desired

\phi=\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}{[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}D]}

Assume
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}D]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}
(i.e.
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\top\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
).

Base Case

: First, let us show that
\mathfrak{D}_{0}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\top\Rightarrow D]]
. We let
C\in\Lambda_{\pi^{\prime}}(\delta)
for
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$})
, and we show that
D\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
for
\pi\in\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
.

(1)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{\prime}}$}}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by assumption and
Lemma

9

(2)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[\{a\}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{\prime}}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow C]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
for some
\mathsf{s^{\prime}}
such that
\pi\in\sigma_{0}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$})
, by construction and the assumption that
C\in\Lambda_{\pi^{\prime}}(\delta)

(3)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[\{a\}\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by rule F.1, (1) and (2), thus
D\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta^{\prime})
as desired

From this we obtain that if
\pi\in\Pi
then
\mathfrak{D}_{0}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\top\Rightarrow D]]
by the semantics.

Inductive Step

: Now, we assume that
\mathfrak{D}_{i}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\top\Rightarrow D]]
and show that
\mathfrak{D}_{i+1}\models\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s^{\prime}}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s}}}[\top\Rightarrow D]]
. Consider
\delta\in\Delta_{i+1}\setminus\Delta_{i}
(the case of
\delta\in\Delta_{i}
is trivial). Thus,
\delta
has been introduced to satisfy some axiom
\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{t}}}[E\sqsubseteq\exists R.F]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
with
\hbox{$\mathsf{t}$}\in\sigma_{i+1}(\pi^{*})
. We must show that if
C\in\Lambda_{\pi^{\prime}}(\delta)
then
D\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
for all
\pi\in\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$})
and
\pi^{\prime}\in\sigma_{i+1}(\hbox{$\mathsf{s}$})
. We consider the three cases.

Case 1

: Assume
\pi^{\prime}=\pi^{*}
, hence
\hbox{$\mathsf{s^{\prime}}$},t\in\sigma^{-1}_{i+1}(\pi^{\prime})
and
C\in\Lambda_{\pi^{\prime}}(\delta)
.

(1)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[F\Rightarrow C]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
from
C\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
, rule XXX and
Lemma

9

(2)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{\prime}}$}}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by assumption and
Lemma

9
, hence
D\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
if
\pi=\Pi\setminus\pi^{*}
as desired

(3)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[C\Rightarrow\top]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by axiom T.4

(4)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[E\Rightarrow\top]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by axiom T.4

(5)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[E\Rightarrow\top]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by (3), (4) and rule C.2

(6)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{\prime}}$}}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[E\Rightarrow\top]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by (5)
Lemma

9

(7)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{\prime}}$}}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[E\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, from (2), (6) and rule C.1, hence
D\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
if
\pi=\pi_{\delta^{\prime},\mathord{\mathop{\lozenge}\nolimits_{\mathsf{s_{1}}}}E}
as desired

(8)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[C\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by (2) and rules I.1

(9)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{\prime}}$}}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[C\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by (8) and
Lemma

9

(10)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{\prime}}$}}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[F\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by (1), (9) and rule C.1, hence
D\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
if
\pi=\pi^{*}
as desired

Case 2

: Assume
\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi_{i}\setminus\{\pi^{*}\}
, and
C\in\Lambda_{\pi^{\prime}}(\delta)

(1)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[G\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{\prime}}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow C]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
from
C\in\Lambda_{\pi^{\prime}}(\delta)
for some
{G\in\mathrm{Con}(F,\pi^{*})}
by construction

(2)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{\prime}}$}}}}[C\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by assumption and
Lemma

9

(3)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[G\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[\top\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, by (1), (2) and rule F.1, hence
D\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
if
\pi=\Pi\setminus\pi^{*}
as desired

(4)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[G\Rightarrow\top]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by axiom T.4

(5)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[E\Rightarrow\top]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
by axiom T.4

(6)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[G\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{*}}}[E\Rightarrow\top]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}

(7)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{\prime}}$}}}}[G\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[E\Rightarrow\top]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}

9

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{\prime}}$}}}}[G\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi}$}}}}[E\Rightarrow D]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
, from (3), (7) and rule C.1, hence
D\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)
if

(9)

\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{u}}}[\top\sqsubseteq\mathord{\mathop{\square}\nolimits_{\mathsf{\hbox{$\mathsf{s}_{\pi^{*}}$}}}}[F\Rightarrow G]]\in\text{$\mathcal{K}$}^{\vdash}
from 1 by construction and

(10)

from (3), (9) and rule C.2

(11)

, by (10) and rules I.1, hence
D\in\Lambda_{\pi}(\delta)

from (12), (14) and rule C.4

, from (11) and

9

(18)

if
